Even, without precedent, we are going to support another amendment by Esquerra which seeks to ensure that members of the Constitutional Court are not seconded to any political party. Let me tell you that we find it incoherent to say that the adoption of this bill will alter constitutional balances while at the same time calling for non-compliance with the decisions of the Constitutional Court. We find it incoherent to recognize that the Constitutional has the power to annul the laws and at the same time to celebrate that it does not have the capacity to enforce its own resolutions, as we find it incoherent to affirm that our system already has the means to prevent non-compliance with the Constitutional resolutions and at the same time to strive not to apply the same system to non-compliance. It is curious, in any case, how some groups who said that with a 51% majority of the seats in an autonomous parliament such as Catalan can declare independence deny this Parliament legitimacy to modify the capacity of the Constitutional Court to enforce its resolutions with the same percentage or more. Mr ÁLVAREZ SOSTRES: Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, a few days ago this House approved the proposal for an organic law for the enforcement of the Constitutional Court's resolutions as a guarantee of the rule of law. Many have been critical of this reform both in form and substance. On that occasion I said that the forms have not been appropriate. This debate should have taken place earlier, further away from the end of the legislature, without the urgency that arises from the procedure itself and as a bill that would always entail the necessary reports of the Council of State and the General Council of the Judiciary; ultimately, from a debate not to rebuttal of a radicalized electoral scenario. So far, okay, but if we go to the bottom of the issue, we do agree that reform is timely because it is a far-reaching initiative for the democratic building in the light of the experience of these years of constitutional development and the sovereignist political dynamics. The truth is that we have witnessed a systematic breach of many Constitutional Court resolutions in order to achieve political objectives of secessionist court, perhaps the most serious, though probably not the most ardent, violations of the school world, of the educational world, in the matter of organic laws with the indolent and irresponsible attitude of socialist governments and governments of the People's Party. In the world of school, ladies and gentlemen, it is the germ of all or almost all the countervalues of this sovereignist spirit, of this secessionist spirit, opposed to the value of the unity of Spain, opposed to the value of the common homeland and of the Spanish being. I thought it appropriate to present two amendments to this text. The first concerns the supplementary scope of the rule. It is not well understood that, in order to ensure the effectiveness of the enforcement of resolutions, the supplementary application of the Dispute-Administrative Jurisdiction Act, which may be the slowest rule in the legal system, is available. It is a paradox that seems opportune, appropriate to suppress. The second concerns the scope of provisional suspension, increasing the individual guarantees of the suspended person with the hearing procedure in relation to the right to defence and effective judicial protection provided for as a right, as you well know, in article 24 of the Constitution. It seems most appropriate if we are talking in addition to a court that understands and guarantees rights and freedoms. It has not been considered - or I have not been told that it has been considered - this possible improvement, but in any case the initiative is too important to reject it simply. Thank you very much, ladies and gentlemen. Thank you very much, President.

You speak of the possibility of being punished on an unheard-of basis when circumstances of particular constitutional significance arise, and this is cooked and eaten by the Government of the State and the Constitutional Court itself without the hearing of the parties and without the hearing of the person to be punished. This is simply contrary to any rule of law and is a genuine abuse. Apart from the fact that there is absolutely no separation of powers when what they are seeking is a substitute execution to make the Constitutional Court the executing arm of the Government, or vice versa, because they also say: the Constitutional Court may propose the replacement execution of the relapsed resolutions by requiring the cooperation of the Government of the nation, in order to take the necessary measures in the prescribed terms. A court cannot entrust a function of its own to the executive branch; this, of course, goes beyond the concept of assistance that could be provided for in the organic law. What you are doing is that you are also permanently burdening yourself with the separation of powers, all of which is as if there is no longer any confidence in the impartiality of the Constitutional Court. Let us give you the reasons that we have given, you are going to move forward as you have done most of the times during this parliamentary term which is fortunately already ending, you are going to continue to apply your roller unilaterally, even if you have the whole House giving them legal or even political reasons not to do so. It is curious the conclusion they draw on the results of the last elections and it is curious that votes are beginning to be counted when a referendum has not been agreed and a referendum has been held; if votes were to be counted, it would have been best to have a referendum and I think we are still in time for that. But let us conclude that the winning list has actually lost and let us not draw the conclusion that the People's Party and the proposals of the President of the Government, and therefore the proposals of the Spanish Government in Catalonia, have received a sound failure, seems to me to be a contradiction. Have you or have you not received a sound failure? I will even add to them - I don't know what they will say, I imagine it won't make any fun of them - the votes of Citizens, and yet that would mean that their policy gets a sonorous failure, because there is 60% that is in favour of it being able to decide and agree that Catalonia is a nation and can be voted on. Keep this in mind. Here, on the basis of order and command and on the basis of moving to the courts, they will get absolutely nothing. Here there is a political problem and political problems - as the Constitutional Court also said in the resolution on the declaration as a nation of Catalonia - have to be resolved through dialogue and political negotiation. They cannot be left in the hands of the courts. What the last election to the Parliament of Catalonia has shown is that there is a political problem and that there is a need for dialogue, that we need to talk and that there is a vast majority of the citizens of Catalonia who do not agree with the policies being implemented by the Spanish Government. Make policy and do not engage in changing the legal nature of the Constitutional Court. Thank you very much, Mr President. [[Applauds]]
ParlaMint-ES_2015-10-01-CD151001.u28	Thank you very much, Mr. Esteban. Mrs Díez has the floor for the Parliamentary Group on Progressive Union and Democracy.
ParlaMint-ES_2015-10-01-CD151001.u29	Thank you, President. On October 1, 1931, in the rostrum of the Congress of Deputies a woman, Clara Campoamor, defended universal suffrage. In short, it is not a very different issue from the one underlying the debate we are having this morning because what was at the time was to implement something as essential in democracy as what is of all we decided among all. In that case it was with the participation of women, but ultimately the fund was the same: what is of all and what affects the whole society we decided among all. It seems to me to be a good way to get closer to the debate that we are really having today, the one that underlies it. Formally, this is about a reform of the Organic Law of the Constitutional Court; a reform whose text, presented by the People's Party, as your ladies and gentlemen know because we have had the opportunity to put it, we do not share it, and that is why our group has tabled an amendment to the alternative text and partial amendments. Formally, this is about a reform of the Organic Law of the Constitutional Court. But ultimately it is - and it is only necessary to listen to the interventions that have preceded mine - that all citizens must be subject to the law, the rulers and the governed. It goes without saying that no one, the thousands or millions of citizens who vote for him, is above the law. It goes from that, so essential, so elementary in democratic terms. On the contrary, the more voters and the more responsible a public representative has, the more he must exercise that responsibility and, therefore, not exercise it and not be respectful of the laws that have placed him at the place where he is supposed to be an aggravating factor. It goes, ladies and gentlemen, that the application of the laws is not negotiated, that goes, too, and there is nothing more to hear than some of the things we have heard before. It goes on that the law is debated before it is passed, everything is discussed and, of course, discussed in order to change it. But while it is in force, its application is automatic, it is applied by judges, of course, but it is applied without discussion and without submitting to any kind of consensus, because there is no consensus to apply the laws. They must be implemented, as must the judgments of the courts. It is, in fact, that without law there is no democracy. For a long time - not only with the so-called Catalan theme or with the current debate - I have heard a lot in Spain say that only the law does not work. It is obvious that politics requires many other necessary actions, among others, to make laws because laws are also political, we have made them, they are part of our responsibility. Of course, not just the law, but out of the law, nothing. The application of the law is part of the things to be done, of course, but outside the law, absolutely nothing. Why now this debate? This debate now comes, in our view, for two reasons. On the one hand, because in Spain there are despotic rulers in Catalonia. A despotic government is the one who believes that it is above the law, that it declares itself above the law. A despotic government whose President-in-Office said yesterday that failing to comply with the rulings of the courts - the two rulings of the Constitutional Court - is an act of democratic rebellion. No, it's not an act of democratic rebellion. In a democracy and in a rule of law not to comply with court decisions, from a criminal standpoint, it is a crime. In a democracy and in a rule of law not to comply with the decisions of the courts, from a political point of view, it is a democratic coup d'état. That's what we're talking about. And that's why we're here today, because there are rulers who believe that failing to comply with the laws and bypass the rulings of the Constitutional Court is an act of democratic rebellion. No, it is a criminal offence and a coup d'état from a political point of view. There are political parties - and it has been seen here - who believe that they can change laws and the Constitution itself without respecting their procedures and we are also here right now doing this debate because the party that proposed this reform says that we do not have sufficient instruments in our legal system to force all citizens to respect the laws and comply with the rulings of the courts. It is not our view, our parliamentary group believes that it is not laws or legal instruments that have been lacking in Spain to force the rulers to comply with the laws, in the same way that the rulers are obliged. It is not a lack of laws or of instruments, but a lack of courage to apply them, not only in the Government that is at the head of the country today, but also in previous governments. There is no lack of laws - I insist -, there are no instruments, but courage to apply those laws and instruments - then I will reflect on the thread of the last thing that has happened in this rostrum - and, therefore, my parliamentary group initially considers - I insist - the same thing: First, that the proposal is wrong and, second, that it is unnecessary but, as we are in the process, we have wanted to adopt, as always, a positive attitude. That is why we have tabled amendments: one in all and the other in part. The most important of our amendments is that one of them proposes the creation of a new offence in the Criminal Code, a new article which expressly states - I insist, which expressly states - that non-compliance with the decisions of the Constitutional Court will be a crime, although there is already, as you know, the generic offence of disobedience and would apply, moreover, which would say a Catalan, the express offence in this case of non-compliance with the decisions of the Constitutional Court. A second amendment proposes the reform of the Organic Law of the Judiciary, instructing the Second Chamber of the Supreme to understand the offences of non-compliance with the decisions of the Constitutional Court; a national court, in sync with the Constitutional Court, are the offences to be prosecuted and can act on a preferential and urgent basis, so it will be effective and we will avoid by that means the discrepancy of criteria. Finally, the most important amendment of the three we are proposing is a reform of the Lecrim to apply the temporary suspension of employment and office to employees and public offices. Ladies and gentlemen, if the application to elected posts and to public employees of the temporary suspension of employment and office was in force, we would have saved ourselves many problems, as it applies in Spain to judges and magistrates because it is in law and as it applies to officials because it is also in law. Since it applies in Spain to officials and judges and magistrates, why can such a provisional suspension not apply to elected posts? Why doesn't it apply to prevent the damage being done when the sentence comes out? If he is then found innocent, he is reappointed to his post, such as judges, judges and officials. I insist that it is legislation that is in force in Spain and what we propose to you is in force in the laws of many other countries, of many other democracies in the world. Finally, in our view, what has been lacking has been political will and not instruments, but, as we are prepared to strengthen existing instruments to protect citizens and democracy, we have tabled these amendments. We want it and we can do it: to strengthen democracy, legal certainty, the instruments to ensure respect for the law and the enforcement of judgments without distorting the Constitutional Court. This is very important. More can be done than is being done without distorting the Constitutional Court from the point of view of its jurisdictional role and from the point of view of what is not its political role. Without denaturalizing it in either way you can have more and better instruments, more guarantee and more effective. I conclude, ladies and gentlemen, with the reflection with which the debate began this morning and which everyone has brought to this rostrum. My group also cares and is concerned about approaching that issue, which is at the heart of the debate. Yes, there is a problem in Catalonia; not a problem with Catalonia or Catalans, but a problem in Catalonia and Catalans who do not want to respect the laws or the judgments of the courts. There's no problem with Catalans who are independenceists or at least I don't have it. I have an alternative proposal and I will always discuss and try to convince them that this is not good for the Catalans or for the Spanish as a whole. The problem we have with one part of the Catalans divides us and places us on the side of the law and the other. That is the most important problem that we have - the real problem - a problem that is difficult to solve, unless the decisions of the courts and the rule of law are complied with. There is a problem, ladies and gentlemen, and I should like to stress this, that we have not created those who defend that no one is above the law. No, we who advocate that no one is above the law are not the ones who have created the problem, but those who confuse cultural identity with political citizenship, and that confusion is precisely at the basis of nationalism. It is a problem that we have not created that we say that there are no Catalan citizens, that there are no Basque citizens or that there are no Madrid or Galician citizens; this is what Spanish citizens are called to be censuses of these autonomys. The problem has not been created by those who think so, because that is the truth. It is the Spanish Constitution that proclaims us all citizens, and then we live in one or another part of Spain. Of course, there is a plural Spain, now that there is so much talk of dialogue, of including plurality and of respecting it. Of course, there is a plural Spain and there is a plural Catalonia. That plurality of Spanish society, that diversity we defend, we also defend it in Catalonia and elsewhere in Spain; a plural Catalonia which must also be preserved and which must also be protected. Of course, diversity is a wealth in democracy, of course it is, provided that we do not confuse the right to diversity with diversity of rights, because that is another issue that is at the heart of this debate, I repeat, provided we do not confuse diversity with diversity of rights, to call it a clear Spanish, with privileges, with the right to have privileges for whatever reason you want to add. Of course, ladies and gentlemen, among those who defend the rule of law, the vast majority of those who are here, there are strong differences and differences inherent in a democracy. If we all thought the same thing, we would not live in a plural and democratic society. And of course, among those who defend the application of laws and the respect of judgments there are strong discrepancies: ideological differences, strategies, tactics, model of state, model of country; there are many differences, naturally and fortunately, but the only line that divides us, the only line that separates us here in the Courts, in the whole of Spain and in Catalonia too, is that which separates those who are on the side of those who want to defend the law, even if it is to change it but within the law and procedures, and those who are on the other side of the line, those who want to skip the laws and change them to their free will and judgment, without respecting the procedures. That is what divides us essentially and fundamentally, and until we realize that, that the only thing that separates us is that there are those who are with the law and those who are over the law or believe in the law, we will not be able to address a debate in depth to find the true solution, the democratic solution. That is why, now that we are also talking so much about immobilism, about the need to negotiate, about what needs to be talked about, I will tell you that it is clear that we need to talk, that we need to negotiate and we need to move. My parliamentary group and I are willing to talk and negotiate everything; how to skip the law, no. And warning: we are radically against, we declare ourselves radically intransigent with those who want to skip the law, without any kind of doubt or alibi, without any kind of complex. As in that verse of Benedetti: in that we are a lost case. We declare ourselves to be radically partial, we are on the side of the law, because the law is democracy, because the law is the guarantee of the rule of law and democracy, and because, in fact, being the law guarantees the rule of law and democracy is what protects and protects all citizens: those who want an independent Catalonia and those who want the independence of another part of Spain as well. Only with the law in hand, changing the law, if we all decide to change it, is it possible to continue living in democracy. This is old, ladies and gentlemen, and it is a shame to have to insist on it again at this stage. This is from Aristotle, who already distinguished between democracy and demagogy. He explained well what demagoguery meant: a strategy used to achieve political power through appeals to prejudices, emotions, fears and hopes of the public to gain popular support through the use of propaganda. No, democracy with capitals, from respect for the law and without complex to defend it and to discuss it. In that, this parliamentary group is absolutely and radically intransigent. That is why we are proposing these amendments to the Group of the European People's Party, because we believe that there are sufficient instruments; we believe that they can be improved, if they need to be improved, but they need to be improved with legal guarantees, because this must also be done by respecting the laws and by respecting our highest body of interpretation of the Constitution, which is the Constitutional Court. That is why we have put these amendments to you, and that is why we would like you to accept them. I conclude, Mr President, with a very brief reflection on the issue that we have been dealing with a moment ago.

ParlaMint-ES_2015-10-01-CD151001.u40	Thank you very much, Mr Coscubiela. Mr Sánchez Amor has the floor, as set out by the speakers themselves, by the Socialist Group.
ParlaMint-ES_2015-10-01-CD151001.u41	Thank you for your flexibility, Mr President, and thank you to Mr Campuzano for the same, and I apologize to him and to the colleague of the People's Party for having to leave immediately after my speech. We are at a time of the legislature that invites us to take stock, and this proposal by the People's Group puts us on the table to make one about whether our democratic institutions have better health or more legitimacy now than four years ago. I don't think so. I believe that the PP, not by having an absolute majority but by having an absolute majority, has made a use of it which has contributed to undermining the already misguided public image and legitimacy of our institutions. It is paradoxical, because it is precisely the fact of having an absolute majority that would advise us to act with complete calm because, after all, all the votes will be won. In the case of the PP it has been the opposite: the absolute majority - which in theory does not need shortcuts - has made use of all kinds of trichinets, forced interpretations, abuse of procedures, run-down deadlines and many other contempts for the regular functioning of the institutions. It will not be necessary to insist on the ubiquitous abuse of decree-laws, to force meetings on the weekend of the Council of State, the use of urgent procedures, to use the Senate to introduce amendments that have nothing to do with the subject in question, to propose party candidates in constitutional bodies and even exceptional regulations, such as that used at the beginning of the process of this own initiative. This situation of progressive deterioration, of erosion of the legitimacy of the institutions, leads me to make this reflection. Ladies and gentlemen, at a historic time when it is so difficult to agree on the great ideological narratives of the background, in a world without strong and shared ideological aspects, suddenly the quality of the institutions and the security of the procedures emerge as one of the few islands of certainty for the citizen and for society. We can no longer be sure of possessing the absolute truths of religion or of classical totalizing ideologies, not even of a supposed universal natural right, so the truth with lowercases must be built repeatedly through the prefixed procedures and within the institutions. The only thing that allows us to ensure that we are approaching that truth, even if it is provisional and historical, is to review whether we have come to that conclusion, to that truth in small numbers, by means of a rational dialogue in which we have used the procedures set out within the institutions. This constitutional patriotism, this patriotism of procedures is becoming one of the few safe havens of a modern democracy without mostly shared accounts. That is why respect for the role and play of the institutions and the strict follow-up of these procedures are so important in any democracy that they assure us of a certain socially shared truth. That's what has been eroding throughout the legislature with the poor attitude of the PP in achieving its short-term political objectives. It is a Panzerdivision model or, in more chaste terms, a tank model. Hernando, take the San Jerónimo Brigade to me and take that hill, and without making prisoners; and the PP sets in motion the picker of democratic procedures and searches the rules to seek the most unexpected and favorable interpretations of their interests, leaving, of course, along the path jirones of legitimacy of the institutions and a certain astonishment of the citizenry. Institutions seem to be plasticin monigotes in the increasingly nervous hands of an absolute majority who knows that it will cease to be. That is why the haste, the lack of consideration and the push we have experienced at the end of this parliamentary term. [[Several Members: Very well!-Applause]] I would like to respond calmly - and I hope objectively - to the five fundamental objections that have been opposed to the reform that we are dealing with here. The first objection is that the reform is unnecessary and it is said that it is unnecessary because the Criminal Code, the criminal procedure, the Constitutional Court, the legal or judicial mechanisms of ordinary aid are perfectly sufficient to address these types of issues and resolve these types of incidents. As far as the criminal issue is concerned, it is enough to say that the legal consequence of the commission of any offence is not the execution of the resolution which is intended to violate but rather the sanction. It's not a substitute, it's complementary. One thing is a precautionary measure that seeks to ensure that a decision is enforced and another is the sanction of a court, which is the legal reproach for failure to comply. So, they're completely different things, they're complementary, they're not alternatives. Secondly, precautionary measures exist in the rest of the legal system with complete normality. Thirdly, as regards the way of the prosecutor's office as an alternative to the reform that we introduced, it is enough to remember that on November 21, 2014, the prosecutor's office initiated and filed a complaint with the Supreme Court of Justice of Catalonia and that ten months later we are as we are. A pseudo-consultation was held, suspended by the Constitutional Court, without the decision being enforceable as a precept, in an act of non-democratic rebellion but of unconstitutional rebellion. As for the channels of ordinary aid, the channels of ordinary aid serve ordinary situations, and you know it, serve for specific breaches of the decisions of the courts, including the Constitutional Court. For which they do not serve, it is for a strategy deployed by the ordinary representative of the State in an autonomous community to liquidate a constitutional State, precisely on the basis of the lack of immediate enforceability of the decisions of the Constitutional Court. Second objection: the Government uses the Constitutional Court not to use article 155 of the Constitution. Mr. Esteban is not here, but, gentlemen of the PNV, you see things. I did not expect you to appeal to 155 to counter the reform we are putting in place. [[Applauds]] It is not a question of the Constitutional Court replacing the Government. It is not that any of the powers constitutionally vested in the Government or the Senate in article 155 pass to the Constitutional Court, no. I remind you that 155 is absolutely intact and available to the Government and Parliament. The issue is that the Constitutional Court will have sufficient precautionary measures to ensure compliance with its judgments and decisions. It is therefore a question of closing a crack that existed in the legal system, especially when from the institutions of the State - because the Generalitat is also an institution of the State - the secessionists pursue the liquidation of the country. It is therefore a matter for the Constitutional Court to be able to give effect to the power granted to it by article 117.3 of the Constitution, the power to enforce the court, which is granted to all courts of all jurisdictional orders, and not only to the judiciary. It is a matter for the Constitutional Court to guarantee something as important for a rule of law as the effective judicial protection of article 24.1 of the Constitution, and it is a matter for the Constitutional Court to guarantee and implement the mandate of 164.1 of the Constitution, that is, that its resolutions have effect against all, also vis-à-vis the President of the Generalitat. Finally, the assumption is in fact completely different. Article 155 refers to illegal, unconstitutional, serious and malicious action for the general interest of Spain, while the reform we introduce concerns a breach, any breach of any kind, in any sector, of the decisions of the Constitutional Court. Third objection: the reform alters the nature of the Constitutional Court. Firstly, the Constitutional Court is not the negative legislator as has been said here. It is much more in Spain than the negative legislator. Not only does the legal system clean up unconstitutional laws, it not only deals with the remedy of unconstitutionality. It also deals with jurisdictional conflicts, amparo remedies, constitutional issues, etc. It's much more than the negative legislator. He is - also in Kelsen's words - the defender of the Constitution and as the defender of the Constitution must concentrate all the necessary resources to ensure the fulfilment of constitutional mandates. In any case, what cannot be done is that a constituted power, such as the Generalitat, can be set up as a substitute for the Constitutional Court and choose the judgments and decisions it must comply with and those that can be jumped to the torera. [[Applauds]] It is not that the Spanish Constitution did not provide for this function of the Constitutional Court to execute its decisions, because the current Organic Law, in its article 92, already contains in an indicative manner, in principle, the possibility that the Constitutional Court may intervene in the execution of its judgments; therefore, it is put in an indicative manner. What we do is articulate and provide much more secure instruments for the Constitutional Court itself to ensure compliance with its resolutions. Thirdly, do you want to tell me that the Austrian Constitutional Court, which is the pure kelsenian, or the German Constitutional Court, of a federal State, in which our Constitutional Court is based, are not democratic? Do you want to tell me that neither of these two federal republics are democratic republics? Do you want to tell me that the fact that the execution of the Constitutional Court's judgments is not regulated in the Constitution prevents its execution, so that nothing that is not in the Constitution can be regulated in this country? Can we not regulate new generation rights, for example, because they are not in the Constitution? A little serious when it comes to tackling the issues. Ladies and gentlemen, if the current Organic Law of the Constitutional Court had already contained this reform, nor would it have reached the Charlotesco non-compliance of the cardboard ballots of 9 November, neither the President could have said now, with absolute bad faith and with a deliberate oceanic ignorance that it is also a hairpin, that the Constitutional Court did not tell him which of its resolutions had to be suspended, and it would not have occurred because already the Constitutional Court would have suspended Mr. Mas, who would not have pulled the ballots out on the street, and today we would not be putting up with the horn, the banana spectacle of seeing a president of an autonomous community and his court of miracles, and there I include them, Mr. Tardà and Mr. Coscubiela, who are together, wrapped up, in full nervous attack, in the flag of Catalonia. It's so rude, it's so sappy, it's so elementary that it produces an indescribable democratic grime, watching you mount this number around the flag of all Catalans. Every time you do that, you bring Catalonia closer to a Bolivarian republic. Every time you do that you are making a mockery of Catalonia's historical, cultural and political reality and you are disrespecting Catalans. ParlaMint-ES_2015-10-01-CD151001.u51	Validation or repeal of Royal Decree-Law 10/2015 of 11 September, granting extraordinary credits and credit supplements in the State budget and taking other measures in the field of public employment and the stimulation of the economy. To present the royal decree-law, the Minister of Finance and Public Administration has the floor. [[The lady]]
ParlaMint-ES_2015-10-01-CD151001.u52	Thank you, Mr. President. Ladies and gentlemen, today we bring to the Congress of Deputies for its validation the Royal Decree-Law 10/2015, of 11 September, granting extraordinary credits and credit supplements in the state budget and taking other measures in the field of public employment and of stimulating the economy. This is a set of measures that require urgent approval, which justifies the Government's choice of this procedure. I would first like to highlight the public employment measures which include this royal decree and which range from the recovery of part of the extra payment withdrawn in December 2012 to the restitution of various days off to officials. As your ladies and gentlemen know, the critical situation in Spain, the Spanish economy at the beginning of the legislature, in 2012, prompted the Government to take numerous initiatives to correct the imbalances that affected us. In this regard, we adopt a comprehensive and complex strategy to combat rapidly, as a shock plan, the high deficit of our public administrations. The deficit was one of the factors that had placed Spain in the face of a scenario that then seemed to inevitably push us to the financial rescue. If that rescue had materialized, it would not only have damaged in a destructive way the image and credibility of Spain, of our economy, but would also have led to the loss of autonomy of government policy and would have had to face extremely serious and profound social cuts as we have seen happening in other European countries. Among the measures taken to reduce public expenditure and thus contribute to the reduction of the deficit, the Government decided to postpone the payment of the December 2012 extra payment to all public workers dependent on the various administrations. On that day of July, when I defended that measure from this rostrum, I explained that the pay was not abolished, that the payment of that pay was moved in time. That is why today I can go back to this rostrum to say that we are complying with what we said at that time and what we have placed, in particular to public officials, to whom we had explained through their trade union representatives that the Government's intention was not to reduce their remuneration, but to postpone in time the collection of their remuneration, as we are now, in 2015, fulfilling, now that we have a scenario of control of the public deficit that is allowing it - as I will now insist - from the figures published two days ago and that, in compliance with the transparency in terms of national accounting of our country's public accounts, they defend the measures that I am proposing to the House today. It had nothing to do with that withdrawal from the December 2012 pay, with what civil servants suffered with the last socialist government, which cut its payroll while freezing public pensions. That was a permanent measure, that was a wage reduction, which was an average of 5% and caused officials to lose their purchasing power. For that reason, the Government already announced that one thing was to postpone the payment of the extra payment and another very different thing was to abolish it and it was not being suppressed in the measures we adopted in this House in July 2012, so that now that the economic situation and the control of the deficit, and also the control of the deficit of the various public administrations, allow it, we are facing that return that we committed at the most difficult time in our country, as it was that summer of 2012. In addition, within this policy of reducing the public deficit, we approved the freezing of the staffing of public sector staff, which had grown too much in the years of economic crisis, in contrast to what was happening with employment in the private sector, where we saw that the destruction of jobs - three and a half million jobs in the previous legislature - contrasted, in terms of worse quality, with the widening of staffing in the public sector. That was simply unfunded and was therefore weighing on the public coffers in a completely wrong policy on the part of the previous socialist government. The freezing of calls for new posts in the Administration, where only a 10% replacement rate was initially allowed in key sectors, was offset by a greater presence of staff also in their jobs. For example, we promote a reduction in the number of leave days for private matters and holidays, so that the internal work of the Administration and public service are as unfettered as possible. This royal decree-law of today corrects those measures adopted in the Public Service, and corrects them when - I insist - the economic conditions and in particular the budgetary conditions allow it, once the economic policy applied these years has allowed to correct, reorient and even make disappear much of the imbalances that forced them to take. Yesterday we knew Spain's balance of payments until July. Yesterday we knew that Spain had a financing capacity vis-à-vis the rest of the world of 2% of gross domestic product, in the same year that our economy is growing thanks to the recovery of domestic demand, and we saw that it is possible to maintain the external financial balance for the first time in our history, making it compatible with the growth of domestic demand in a sustained way, thanks to savings from public administrations, which compensated for the lower savings from the private sector, because obviously also in it the investment is recovering. This is what makes credible and sustainable the economic policy that is leading us to correct our country's economic imbalances. I understand that what I have just summarized is not understandable in terms of great public opinion, but it is the key to the economic change that Spain is experiencing. Precisely the need for financing vis-à-vis the rest of the world, which reached 10% of our gross domestic product in 2007, brought us into the very deep crisis that the then government did not want to recognize. Many analysts also did not want to recognize the consequences of a crisis that was looming over us because of this external deficit, because of this need for Spain's funding vis-à-vis the rest of the world. That is what we are correcting; that is what serves to reduce the public deficit. For this reason, every time we reduce the public deficit we grow more and more jobs are created in Spain and that is why today this decree-law comes to recognize those who have been the architects, the public officials, who have sustained the supply and quality of public services. Now, when we are recording these economic conditions, we can return the effort to these officials and tell them that they have every right to find a reward for having provided so much to turn the Spanish economy around, by the way, at a record time, winning all the forecasts. These days we have seen how rating agencies improve Spain's growth forecasts for this year. All analysts, both inside and outside Spain, review the forecasts of economic growth upwards. If you want to understand this, this royal decree-law comes to correct what we did in July 2012. We did it at the time we had to do it, making decisions that were then the only ones that could get us out of the crisis, as we are effectively checking throughout 2015. Consequently, the economic policy measures contained in this Decree-Law relate first of all to the chapter on budgetary amendments and include, on the one hand, the granting of several extraordinary appropriations and additional appropriations in the State budget amounting to EUR 630.571.641.30. A credit supplement is introduced into the budget of Section 31, amounting to EUR 252 million. This money will be used to finance the recovery of part of that extraordinary pay of public employees for 2012. I remember that the refund of the above-mentioned extra pay began in January of this year and is now going to continue in October in the General Administration of the State. In the first part of October, officials of the General Administration of the State will receive this pay quarter on a payroll other than the general payroll they receive monthly. The return will be completed next year, as foreseen by the General Budgets of the State for the year 2016, which are currently being processed in the Senate. The regulation of this return is of a basic and powerful nature, so that the rest of the public administrations will decide whether to agree on payment according to their economic and financial situation. As I said a few minutes ago, with the return of this new part of the extra payment, the Government is scrupulously fulfilling its commitment to public workers and their trade union representatives. We said that money would be returned and now we're doing it; we're already starting to do it at the beginning of the year. There are those who interpret that this has to do with the fact that we are at the end of the legislature, but with what it has to do - I insist - it is with economic recovery, which is a tangible reality that did not exist before; it has to do with the improvement of the margin of public finances, in particular the state finances, which today can make these disbursements. In addition, this new situation, which includes increased collection, has allowed to advance to July part of the tax rebate that we had programmed by law that would enter into force in the IRPF in January 2016 and that is already being applied to payrolls, pensions, retentions of civil servants, pensioners and private sector workers. At this point in the year we can say that it has fully entered into force. How are the state budgets supporting these decisions being conducted? We knew this very week the result of the state's position in terms of national accounts closed in August. August already incorporated in the payrolls of the workers of the General Administration of the State the decrease of the IRPF, the second of the year, which translates into a new tariff and therefore in the new deductions to the decrease of the IRPF. How is the state's collection going in the period from January to August? The increase in this tax collection, in terms of national accounts, is 6.7 %. It should be borne in mind that from January to August, through the tax reduction, we have injected into taxpayers' pockets of the order of 3 billion euros and tax collection is rising - I insist - in terms of national accounts almost 7 %, which shows that we are doing the budgetary policy that we did in the times when we were government before, the policy that we did in the years 1999 and 2003, a policy that is working again to stimulate economic activity, to grow, to create employment and to broaden the tax base. Ladies and gentlemen, this is a very relevant fact, which of course has not appeared in any medium of communication. The headlines have spoken of the deficit of the autonomous communities. I insist from the rostrum to try to convince someone of the importance of correctly reading the data that our national accounts publish every month and inviting the media to reflect the real evolution of our country's national accounts. This is what sustains that economic change, which makes the economic policy that we are implementing consistent and protects measures like this that we are moving forward. In particular, in August the deficit of the State - State component - stood at 2.53% of gross domestic product. This means that, compared to the first eight months of the previous year, in the period January-August 2014 the government deficit fell by no less than 21,9 %, almost 22 %, taking into account that we had already paid the first part of the payment in January. This budgetary framework - 22% reduction in the state deficit - has also not merited any headlines, but we must explain it, we must be in it. All the headlines have been that the autonomous communities do not comply, but they are complying with the State and by fulfilling and explaining why they do we are anticipating the financing of the other subsectors of the Spanish State, of the Spanish Administration. It is very relevant what is happening, because what we are reading is the behavior of all the public administrations of Spain. Now, with this data, with these facts, we make decisions and we are sure that these decisions, which are on the one hand more expenditure - the return of pay - will be consistent with the objectives of reducing the public deficit, as we are getting month by month. As for the days of leave, the days known as days of free disposal, this royal decree restores the situation that existed in 2011, both in permits for particular matters and in permits for particular matters by seniority and in the additional days of vacation by seniority. We can do that, I insist, once we have changed the replacement rate for this year and reached a total number of public employees in Spain similar to that we had in 2004. We have gone to 100 per cent replacement rates of public staff and, once we have implemented them, we can return that reduction in the number of days of free disposal, of holidays in a totally saved way and consistent with our public policy approaches. And I'd like to insist on the credit supplement I've described. I am talking about credit supplement because it has been understood something like that the Government of Spain had to make a debt issue in order to repay the extra payment. No, we have included it in the deficit targets, that is, we do it because the State, the General Administration of the State, is going to achieve the planned deficit target altogether. This is not how it has spread and this is very important in the political debate with the autonomous communities, with local governments and so on. We are doing so when we have met our deficit target and are therefore doing so as we have always done, guided and coherently. What we're enabling is a credit supplement. Returning to the budgetary changes, I have to note that, together with the credit supplement dedicated to the refund of the extra payment, there are extraordinary credits and a credit supplement of EUR 262 million for the Ministry of Development. Most of this money corresponds to prior years' obligations amounting to EUR 251.3 million. These are investments undertaken by the Roads Directorate-General, which affect expropriations, interest on delays, price revisions and final certifications and liquidations. In addition, an additional EUR 31 million is intended to finance higher costs than initially foreseen in the actions carried out by the Society of Rescue and Maritime Safety to combat the pollution of the ship Oleg Naydenov, which know that it sank in the vicinity of the Canary Islands. It also includes extraordinary credits and credit supplements for €76 million to meet obligations from previous years of the Ministry of Agriculture, Food and Environment. Of this amount, 40 million correspond to IBI's payment of state-owned reservoirs and other assets, and 36 million to certifications, price revisions, expropriations and other investments of the Directorate-General for Water. Finally, this royal decree-law contains an extraordinary credit in the budget of the Ministry of Defence, for the amount of 20 million euros, destined for the acquisition of tactical and logistic vehicles. It should be borne in mind that the Spanish army actively participates in missions abroad to combat terrorism and peacekeeping, and that in many of these certain armoured vehicles are needed. The budgetary restrictions of these years have meant that the available vehicles have been used intensively, to the limit of their operating cycle, and it is therefore necessary at this time to acquire ninety-two of these vehicles, both in order to allocate them to the missions and to be able to train in the handling of them to the personnel framed in the successive relays. This Royal Decree-Law also amends Law 7/1965 of 2 April, which regulates the bases of local government, to ensure greater control of public accounts in small municipalities. The local reform adopted in this legislature has been a very important step in controlling the finances of local entities by empowering professional officials to carry out this work. In recent years, the Government has promoted the convening of extraordinary job offers to provide these positions, but the number of vacancies is very high and there is no room for replacing all employees who are doing these jobs from night to morning with new professionals with national qualifications. This royal decree-law gives a transitional solution to this problem that arises in local entities of municipalities with less than 5,000 inhabitants, which are also the ones with the least economic resources. The solution is that local actors and secretaries can carry out these treasury control functions in an additional way; in this way, this important task is placed in the hands of officials to continue the professional and independent control of our municipal finances. On the other hand, ladies and gentlemen, the royal decree we bring to the House seeks to help entities that are at financial risk so that they can meet their payment obligations. Today it is known and acknowledged that in the last four years local entities have made a great effort to reduce their debt and their public deficit. This expenditure control effort has led our local administration to have a budget surplus and is working very actively to achieve the deficit targets agreed with the European Union. However, there are 219 local entities facing serious financial problems, which has led the Government to take extraordinary measures in this legislature to help them move forward. These financial problems harm other entities, such as suppliers and contractors working for these municipalities, who do not charge in time and form their invoices due to the lack of liquidity of the above-mentioned entities. It should be borne in mind that if we do not solve this problem, there is a risk that these local entities will be bankrupt or that their suppliers will stop working for them, which would cause their neighbours to cease to have essential services such as garbage collection, road light or water supply. As you know, one of the concerns of the government in this legislature has been that all administrations pay their bills in a maximum amount of time, so as not to harm so many suppliers, so many self-employed and so many businesses - small and medium-sized, many of them, but also large - that work for the public sector. To achieve this, initiatives such as the Supplier Payment Fund and other liquidity mechanisms were taken, in addition to setting maximum payment periods for outstanding invoices. In this line, the Royal Decree-Law approves the granting of advances on the participation of the affected municipalities in the revenues of the State of 2016, the maximum amount of which will be the amount that they received on account for this concept in 2015. These advances must necessarily be used to cancel outstanding debts with suppliers and contractors in order to reduce the average payment period to suppliers to a maximum of 30 days. Local entities will be required to return this money through loan operations that will be formalised in June 2016 from the ten-year-old local funding fund's behaviour. With this money these local entities will also have to deal with the execution of final judgments and the payment of outstanding public debts with the Tax Agency and the General Treasury of Social Security. If all the municipalities concerned requested the corresponding advance for their maximum amount it would entail a disbursement of EUR 593,8 million. Those municipalities which are at risk because of their financial situation and which have acceded to or acceded to the local authority management fund compartment in 2015 may also, on an exceptional basis, request, before 15 October, the formalisation of loans from that compartment to cancel the outstanding public debt with the Tax Agency and the General Treasury of Social Security, which is currently being compensated by the application of withholding taxes on State tax participation. This measure could cover a maximum of EUR 212 million. As I said to your ladies and gentlemen, the fight against the lateness that we have undertaken from the government, which includes these measures to which I referred, has had important benefits for companies that contract with the public administration but has contributed fundamentally to the decoupling of our economy. The money injected to deal with outstanding invoices, besides helping these businesses - as I said, mostly SMEs and self-employed - to move forward in times of crisis has served to stimulate economic activity. However, on numerous occasions, self-employed and small businesses have called for other initiatives that would help to reduce the backlog in the private sector. In the same vein, the European Commission has called on the Kingdom of Spain to take measures to reduce the payment periods for this sector, as it has already done in the public sector. The Government continues to work on the search for ways to indirectly encourage large companies to pay their debts to self-employed and other companies within a maximum period of time. Now, the Public Sector Contracts Act requires that a company be able to contract with the public administration to prove its economic and financial capacity. To this end, contracting authorities may require an annual turnover, the existence of occupational risk insurance or a net worth figure. From now on, with this new law, another possible requirement is added: to include the average period of payment to suppliers as another element for crediting economic and financial solvency. In this way, any contracting authority of the Administration may leave out of its award to large undertakings which do not pay their suppliers within a maximum period of time. The Government considers that lateness is a serious obstacle to economic recovery in which we are immersed as a country, as this lateness makes suppliers unable to pay in turn to other actors in the production chain or obliges them to be indebted unnecessarily, with the consequent losses of efficiency and competitiveness and with the highest financial costs, both for themselves and for the economy as a whole. Over these years it has been shown that only with calls to pay in time and form - it is good to do so, but its effectiveness is relative, it is not achieved too much - is not enough and that some companies abuse their economic power to impose their terms on smaller and modest suppliers or subcontractors. That is unacceptable and less so at a time when we need to improve the efficiency of our country's economy as a whole, of all economic actors, and the competitiveness and efficiency of all enterprises, starting with small and medium-sized enterprises and self-employed ones; that is what strengthens and drives job creation. The Government has therefore decided to intervene in private relations in this way, albeit indirectly - as I said earlier - to defend the weakest actors against the temptations of abuse of the strongest. Of course, this does not mean that there is a free market; in fact, all parties will be able to continue to act freely in their commercial relations. Large companies - understood as those that cannot draw up short profit and loss accounts - should know that if they do not pay their suppliers in time, they run the risk of not being able to contract with the public administration. Finally, ladies and gentlemen, in order not to extend this presentation further, I will only mention the change that is introduced in the system of access and provision of teaching and research staff positions at public universities. The Royal Decree-Law, approved by the Government, last September 11, amends the Organic Law of Universities to allow access to the body of university professors, through internal promotion, to any member of the body of university professors. In addition, it provides on a permanent basis for the provision of vacancies in a university with professors and professors from other universities, which has already been temporarily authorized by the State General Budgets Act 2015. This solves problems that are raised in the field of our universities and whose origin are the low replacement rates applied during previous years. In addition, the path of internal promotion is strengthened as a substantial part of the professional career of university professors, which will undoubtedly also serve to encourage their work and give greater stability to their important teaching and research role in our society. Ladies and gentlemen, as you can see, this royal decree-law includes important measures for different areas of our economy and of our science and research, whose approval requires the utmost urgency. These are measures that will favour public officials, local authorities with difficulties, small and medium-sized enterprises, self-employed persons and universities, and will also improve certain activities carried out in several ministries. The legislature is coming to an end and we consider it very necessary that all these objective needs that I have raised be met immediately. That is why I ask the House to vote in favour of the validation of this royal decree-law. Thank you, ladies and gentlemen. Thank you, Mr. President. [[Applauds]]

ParlaMint-ES_2015-10-01-CD151001.u54	Thank you, Madam President. That the abuse of the decree-law has been the usual rather than the extraordinary, as our Constitution establishes, cannot lead us to the complacency in this Congress to consider it normal in democratic terms. Separation of powers is a key element in distinguishing a parliamentary system from an absolutist regime, such as that governed by Spain during these four years. The abuse of decree-laws has been accompanied not only in quantitative terms - sixty-six decree-laws so far - but also by a twisting manipulation of their contents, mixing barbecues with merinas, things that are impossible to validate in a single act. In this House there are good specialists in civil procedural law, without going further in the People's Group itself, and at times I have the impression that something is going on which even the Government and the People's Group itself do not take into account these capacities and by some circumstances that I am missing it turns out that they end up leaving the draft laws in the hands of that kind of plague in which some advisers have been turned into Aranzadi brigades, which seem to proliferate in all governments but which in this one have become a real plague, and less so that the lawyers and the services of the House make real efforts to discover barbarities such as some of those that have appeared in the handling of this draft law, texts that arrive in the Senate in which, at best, the same article has two different writings, and it has not happened once, but two. The result is usually what we have, unfortunately, very commonly, laws with very poor legislative techniques. And if that is serious in the area of substantive law, much more so when it comes to the procedural sphere, to the point, I insist, that we have had to address the fact that texts arrive from the Senate to this House in which on two different issues two different wordings appear from the same article. That certainly has an explanation for that poor legislative technique and the way in which laws are being drafted in this country. Then we will see how legal operators and the courts will tell how this law may have such a despicable legal technique. Well, here's the source of some of the things. Referring to those improvements, I would like to stress that it is common sense to extend the three to five days for written replies from legal persons. There are minimum deadlines in which the answer requires a certain calm, and having expanded makes sense. There are others that also have logic, such as considering that the subjection to arbitration does not have the same level as the subjection to mediation, among other things because the arbitration by definition excludes the continuation of the judicial procedure itself - what is incredible is that the text would reach the Senate itself - for example, those issues that establish that the person concerned should be required to appear for notifications, when the law itself states that such notifications will be established by telematic means, it is a bit absurd, and that is something else that the Senate resolves. It is an old age that remained in our law of civil prosecution that disappears and that, if it could be moved to the realm of public administrations, it would perhaps help us to improve its functioning, because, no doubt, at the moment there is a certain disregard for the citizen and especially for the use of his time in the administration of justice, which at the moment is key to the lives of many people, and it also often has very negative impacts in terms of competitiveness because having to travel for a notification that can be made by telematic means is not only an inconvenience for people, but it ends up being a problem without a doubt for the professional environment of the companies for which they work. I take this opportunity to say that if any government ever considered such an unattractive measure from the perspective of the political market but at the same time so useful from the perspective of the democratic quality and the functioning of the administration as a whole as it was to cancel all the obligations to appear in any scenario because everything could be done by telematics, by means of networks, this country could possibly enter definitively into the era of modernity and, above all, it would improve greatly in economic and human terms. And if I may say that it is in aspects like this when one realizes that at times those who draft the laws do not usually have the same domestic and human problems as the rest of their fellow citizens, because otherwise, there are things that would have them very present. I would like to pay particular attention to one of the important amendments which I also think is positive, such as the one concerning so-called unfair clauses and the fact that it is possible for the courts to agree to their consideration on their own initiative. Thankfully, it's never too late if the bliss is good. Let me remind the People's Group that the approach that abusive clauses could be considered ex officio by the courts is an issue that has been raised in relation to evictions not only by many courts but by groups in this House. If we were heard in time, we could sometimes avoid suffering many people. How much suffering could have been avoided if at the beginning of this parliamentary term we had heard social organizations, the courts and some groups in this House when we raised the issue of the abusive clauses that exist in some contracts, those accession contracts that usually make companies that have a position of domination, of total and absolute domination with the users. Unfortunately, that was not the case, and that suffering that we could have avoided has not been avoided by that deafness of the Government and the People's Group. Welcome is that reform which also comes to solve an issue which had even been the subject of some eardropping by the European Union itself. Finally, I would like to insist on a very important issue. Our group has been reiterating for a long time - and I take advantage of the fact that the Minister of Justice is here, whom we thank for his presence, so that he can listen to some things - that the improvement of procedural laws is important for the functioning, such as that of the judiciary. You will agree that the laws require time, calm, calm, tranquillity, just everything that has not happened in this legislature. As I have had the opportunity to talk with some of you in the privacy, which Mr Aznar would say - and in Catalan - I know what you think, but unfortunately what some of you think is not what the Group of the European People's Party Government has done. What has happened in this legislature with the laws in the field of justice is a real disaster. The laws, even the good ones, when handled, end up being a real disaster because no one knows even those that apply. I come from the field of work and can assure you that there is no legal operator in the field of work who currently knows what is in force and what is not in force. It is a good thing that telematics and networks allow legislation to be somewhat harmonised. That's very good; dealing with procedural laws is important but let's not be fooled, the improvement of the functioning of justice will come by that improvement of procedural laws but also by providing the Administration of Justice with sufficient resources to ensure that procedural laws are not a wet paper. Here are some important reforms that have been incorporated - I do not say no - but that can then be left on wet paper because they cannot be implemented, because the reality of the judicial offices does not allow them to start them. In that regard, one might wonder why political elites have historically given so little priority to public investment in justice in the past forty years. I have a double hypothesis, but first I want to ask you a reflection. It is not possible that a country that has been able to set in motion some of the best and most efficient administrations in Europe - recognised by the whole world - such as the General Treasury of Social Security, the INSS, in general the Social Security Administration or the Tax Agency itself - I am not talking now about the political but management aspect - has been profoundly incapable of significantly improving the functioning of the public service of justice. Important things have been done, but not all of them. I put two hypotheses to you, although one of them is the most important. I think the reason is very obvious, the political elites take very much into account the interests of the economic elites and the powerful and - let us not forget - at the moment the economic powers need less and less justice to assert their interests; therefore, justice does not interest them. Justice is what those in position need, not of domination but of inferiority in societies to assert their rights. And that is why the functioning of justice has not been a priority. I do not say this, it was denounced yesterday by the President of Uruguay, Mr Tabaré, at the headquarters of the United Nations at his annual conference when he denounced the situation he is suffering at the moment, as a result of the pressure of the smokers and, in particular, of Philip Morris on the State of Uruguay and the shame that the State of Uruguay must submit its sovereignty to the decision of arbitration mechanisms for the benefit of Philip Morris. By the way, all those who discuss national sovereignty, present and future, would do well to place that debate in those terms of the twenty-first century. I end with a reference, not to the Minister of Justice - who is left with only a few newspapers - but in general to the House and the country. Soon, I hope that the next parliamentary term - I will not see it because this is one of my last speeches in the House - will truly be, among other things, the legislature of justice. Let me tell the People's Party and the Minister of Justice that if the Ministers of Justice of this Government had devoted the same effort to improving justice that they have devoted to imposing their ideological views on us and the interests of certain lobbyists, it might have been possible to make further progress in the public service of justice. The truth is that what you have not done I hope the citizens will do with their vote; if you do not attend to reasons I hope you will attend to the verdict of the ballot box soon enough. Thank you very much.

[[The Lord]] It does not respond to an urgent demand from society, Minister, but to the government's need to facilitate the privatization of basic aspects of civil procedure. Once again, the Government, supported by the absolute majority, imposes on the House for its adoption a bill that, like all its actions in the field of justice, goes either through concessions to certain corporations or through its desire for privatization. They intend to impose a two-speed justice system: one for those who can pay for it and another for the rest, one for the rich and one for others. Now, through the transfer to the prosecutors, they proclaim their status as co-operators of the Administration of Justice, but what they do is, on the one hand, increase the number of procedures or procedures in which the figure of the prosecutor is mandatory, and on the other, to carry out a privatization of basic aspects of the civil procedure and of the guarantees inherent in the costs of communication and assistance, while at the same time weakening any consideration of the prosecutors as co-operators of the Administration of Justice, since they eliminate the possibility that the prosecutors have to carry out acts of communication or cooperation when required by the judicial secretary. In the end the prosecutor ceases to be a collaborator of the court if the secretary cannot impose on him the task. The sole purpose is, therefore, to privatize, privatize without controls a basic part of the functioning of civil justice. Another joint note of the reforms it brings to the House is the return to the past. This is the case with the proposal for a reform of the oral procedure. Its regulation in the Criminal Procedure Act 2000 enshrined constitutional principles in the civil procedure, such as the procedural economy, orality, proximity and concentration; principles to which this reform renounces. In the process of amendments to the Congress, the People's Group tabled forty-four amendments which, on the one hand, sought to make technical corrections to improve a deplorable draft, and which actually entailed an amendment to the full content of the bill. I have already said this in the Committee on Justice and I will repeat it: this constitutes a very clear fraud of law in that it involves the removal of the mandatory reports that must be processed in the adoption of the draft laws, as stipulated in article 22 of the Government's Law 50/1997 of 27 November 1997 and article 561 of the Organic Law on the Judiciary. They also prevent parliamentarians from fully exercising our rights, as soon as we cannot amend those proposals, which also entail complete reforms of laws that are under way, such as the Law on Free Legal Assistance, unilaterally and without withdrawing that draft in progress. They also amend many other laws that are in force. And what is even more relevant is that they do so on a non-existent or false motivation in the amendments, as they speak of technical improvements and on many occasions the precepts are neither improvements nor technical. In the Senate you present twenty-four other amendments which continue to amend other existing laws; laws which, I insist, you yourself are sometimes dealing with, but which you are amending in this way. You have charged yourselves with the principle of legal certainty. Our group tabled 59 amendments in the Senate. We do not accept any amendments in either House. We're probably all wrong, except you. To all this must be combined the null and void modernizing action of the Ministry of Justice in this legislature, which intends to circumvent by imposing on all judicial and judicial professionals the obligation of the use of telematic means from 1 January 2016, as if that were so easy: you pick it up in a law and, finally, the miracle is performed. Another key element in defence of the spurious interests of this reform is the new legal regime for the extinguishing of civil obligations contained in the Civil Code, which had remained unchanged from its promulgation to the present time. The Government has decided to end the more than one hundred and twenty years of immunity from these precepts without any explanation; this in itself makes it unacceptable since they do not provide any justification for the urgency of this legislative amendment. The reform proposal is a frontal attack on the disruptive effectiveness of the creditor's out-of-court claims, which, in my opinion and that of my group, is another major feature of the proposal. It ends with a pen - without any justification in the law or in the reports - with the possibility that the creditor will keep his right alive through successive extrajudicial acts of debt claim, recovering with each of them a new full term of limitation. Even without expressly stating it in fact, what they eliminate altogether is the typically disruptive effect of the extrajudicial claim, because the creditor who uses it will not have a new five-year period to exercise its right, and - more curiously - it also leaves a lot of doubts in the air, such as, for example, whether the creditor's invitation to the debtor to mediate will be considered an extrajudicial claim. What about arbitration, what treatment does it deserve? Will there be suspension of the prescription in such cases or do we talk about interruption or accumulate both effects? No less nonsense is the fifth transitional provision, which regulates an allegedly transitional regime for the limitation of actions born before the reforms, which in fact is not such, because it refers to article 1939 of the Civil Code, a precept that literally refers to the limitation begun before the publication of the code, in order to achieve a certain effect of retroactivity improper of the normative change. That is to say, the new time limit for completing the statute of limitations applies also to the one already initiated, but not yet completed, before the entry into force of the legal reform by cutting to five years, counted from the day on which the new law is applied, the time limits that the creditor may have previously. Faced with this, the Socialist Group's proposals have sought to prevent the privatization of essential elements of civil proceedings and the reform of the oral trial, which is completely disrupted by this reform, under a bochronous pretext for justifying the change in an alleged strengthening of effective judicial protection. We recall that the explanatory statement of the Civil Prosecution Act of 2000 made clear the values that he wanted to impose, and thus stated that the law designs the declaratory processes, so that immediacy, publicity and orality must be effective, in verbal trials because of the significance of the hearing, and in the ordinary because, upon request and reply, the most outstanding procedural milestones are the pre-trial hearing and the trial itself, both with the inexcusable presence of the judge. Well, the proposal is radically contrary. You are going to turn the verbal trial into a written procedure, you are going to get the principles of immediacy, orality and publicity that for the verbal trials recognized the law of the year 2000. To all this is added what the General Council of the Judiciary rightly states, and it is that this measure will also lead to a clear delay in the trial. Finally, I wonder if you really believe that this reform is necessary, if you really believe that you are going to improve the civil process. I believe that you do not believe it yourself, and I also believe that we should also ask ourselves whether it violates the Constitution itself. Surely it does, because it violates the rights of equality, non-discrimination and effective judicial protection that our Constitution protects. That is why we reject this reform, because it is unnecessary, because it does not meet any requirements of Spanish society, of legal professionals or of the plaintiffs of justice, only privatized fundamental aspects of civil procedure. What they are going to achieve is that our justice is slower, more ineffective, more insecure, and ultimately more unjust. [[Applauds]]
